People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, doctor, I came in because there’s been some things happening that my wife says I’m making too much of, but I don’t think so. When I sit with the men at the domino table, they’ll say certain things—not directly to me, you understand, but the way they say it, the timing of it, it feels like they’re giving me little messages. Like warnings, mostly. And sometimes even the radio announcer, just talking like normal, but there’ll be a phrase that lands just so, and I know it’s meant for me.

My wife tells me, “That’s just talk, don’t pay it any mind,” but I can’t shake it. I know when someone is trying to tell me to be careful. I’m old, but I’m not foolish. So I figured I better come tell somebody before it gets… well, before I keep worrying on it by myself.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, sir… I’d have to say it has, though I don’t like admitting that. I used to be regular with the church choir rehearsals, you know, even if my voice isn’t what it used to be. But I’ve missed some because I get to thinking maybe folks are saying things around me, or I don’t feel settled enough to go sit there with everybody.

And my garden too—now that bothers me. I’ve always kept after it, even if it’s just a little patch. But some parts have gotten away from me lately, weeds and all, because I’ll go out there and start thinking on what I heard, turning it over and over, and then I don’t finish what I meant to do.

The domino games… well, that’s maybe the biggest one socially. I used to sit with the men and play, talk a little, laugh some. But I guess I’ve made it uncomfortable, asking what they meant or getting quiet when I think something is aimed my way. So I don’t join in like I used to. I still want to, but it doesn’t feel easy anymore.

Well… I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, doctor. I don’t want to sound like I’m saying everybody’s in on something, because that’s not quite how I mean it. But it does feel connected somehow, like the things I hear at the domino table and then later on the radio, they’re not just random talk.

The men will say something in a certain way, or right after I’ve been thinking about something, and it feels like they’re giving me a warning without saying it straight out. Then the radio announcer might use a phrase that lands the same way, and I think, well, there it is again. My wife says it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe she wants to calm me down, but I know when something is meant for me.

So… a plan? I’m not sure. But I do believe somebody, somehow, is trying to warn me to be careful.

Yes, sir, I do think they’re about me. Not every single word folks say, I’m not saying that, but certain things… the way the men at the domino table say it, or the timing of it, it feels like it’s meant for my ears.

fast that I cannot hold one thought long enough to explain it right.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: If I am trying to read the church paper or talk with my wife, a car outside, a bird, or a sound from the radio pulls me away and I forget what I was doing.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During my bright, restless weeks when I am sleeping little and making big plans, I still hear my friend’s voice and feel the radio or domino men are sending me warnings.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: Since all this started, more months have been taken up by those high-energy spells and their aftereffects than by my calmer stretches.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a quiet month when I was sleeping normally and not full of big plans, but I still heard my friend on the porch and believed the radio was speaking to me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I have missed choir rehearsals, let parts of my garden go, and made the domino games uncomfortable enough that I do not join like I used to.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, since after my close friend passed away.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The voices and special messages have been happening on and off for about 9 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: Those weeks of high energy, little sleep, and big plans have been coming back for about 11 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 84 / not_in_workforce / less_than_9th\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have these messages been affecting your daily life or activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir… I’d have to say it has, though I don’t like admitting that. I used to be regular with the church choir rehearsals, you know, even if my voice isn’t what it used to be. But I’ve missed some because I get to thinking maybe folks are saying things around me, or I don’t feel settled enough to go sit there with everybody.\n\nAnd my garden too—now that bothers me. I’ve always kept after it, even if it’s just a little patch. But some parts have gotten away from me lately, weeds and all, because I’ll go out there and start thinking on what I heard, turning it over and over, and then I don’t finish what I meant to do.\n\nThe domino games… well, that’s maybe the biggest one socially. I used to sit with the men and play, talk a little, laugh some. But I guess I’ve made it uncomfortable, asking what they meant or getting quiet when I think something is aimed my way. So I don’t join in like I used to. I still want to, but it doesn’t feel easy anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are part of a larger plan or scheme against you?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, doctor. I don’t want to sound like I’m saying everybody’s in on something, because that’s not quite how I mean it. But it does feel connected somehow, like the things I hear at the domino table and then later on the radio, they’re not just random talk.\n\nThe men will say something in a certain way, or right after I’ve been thinking about something, and it feels like they’re giving me a warning without saying it straight out. Then the radio announcer might use a phrase that lands the same way, and I think, well, there it is again. My wife says it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe she wants to calm me down, but I know when something is meant for me.\n\nSo… a plan? I’m not sure. But I do believe somebody, somehow, is trying to warn me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are specifically about you or something that's happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir, I do think they’re about me. Not every single word folks say, I’m not saying that, but certain things… the way the men at the domino table say it, or the timing of it, it feels like it’s meant for my ears.\n\nAnd then when the radio announcer says something that fits with it, I can’t just brush that off. My wife tells me it’s only regular talk, and maybe she means well, but I know how it feels when something is being pointed toward me. It’s like they’re warning me to be careful about something in my life, even if they won’t come right out and say it plain.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you believe these messages are unique to you, or could they also apply to someone else in a similar situation?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I suppose… well, doctor, I suppose words can apply to anybody if you look at them one way. Like if a radio man says, “Be careful out there,” I know anybody listening could hear that. I’m not saying the whole world stops for me or anything like that.

But the way it happens with me, it feels more particular than that. The men at the domino table will say something small, maybe nothing much to someone else, but it comes right after something I’ve been worrying on, or they say it in a tone that makes me think, now that was meant for me. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.

My wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.

S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are part of a larger plan or scheme against you?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, doctor. I don’t want to sound like I’m saying everybody’s in on something, because that’s not quite how I mean it. But it does feel connected somehow, like the things I hear at the domino table and then later on the radio, they’re not just random talk.\n\nThe men will say something in a certain way, or right after I’ve been thinking about something, and it feels like they’re giving me a warning without saying it straight out. Then the radio announcer might use a phrase that lands the same way, and I think, well, there it is again. My wife says it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe she wants to calm me down, but I know when something is meant for me.\n\nSo… a plan? I’m not sure. But I do believe somebody, somehow, is trying to warn me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are specifically about you or something that's happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir, I do think they’re about me. Not every single word folks say, I’m not saying that, but certain things… the way the men at the domino table say it, or the timing of it, it feels like it’s meant for my ears.\n\nAnd then when the radio announcer says something that fits with it, I can’t just brush that off. My wife tells me it’s only regular talk, and maybe she means well, but I know how it feels when something is being pointed toward me. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.\n\nMy wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever think that maybe you're misinterpreting these messages, or that they might not be meant for you after all?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well… I’ve tried to tell myself that, doctor. I have. Especially when my wife says, “You’re making too much of it, it’s just talk,” I’ll sit there and think, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m hearing it wrong. I don’t want to be a foolish old man seeing things where there’s nothing.

But then it happens again. The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.

So yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.

little and making big plans, I still hear my friend’s voice and feel the radio or domino men are sending me warnings.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: Since all this started, more months have been taken up by those high-energy spells and their aftereffects than by my calmer stretches.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a quiet month when I was sleeping normally and not full of big plans, but I still heard my friend on the porch and believed the radio was speaking to me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I have missed choir rehearsals, let parts of my garden go, and made the domino games uncomfortable enough that I do not join like I used to.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, since after my close friend passed away.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The voices and special messages have been happening on and off for about 9 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: Those weeks of S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are specifically about you or something that's happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir, I do think they’re about me. Not every single word folks say, I’m not saying that, but certain things… the way the men at the domino table say it, or the timing of it, it feels like it’s meant for my ears.\n\nAnd then when the radio announcer says something that fits with it, I can’t just brush that off. My wife tells me it’s only regular talk, and maybe she means well, but I know how it feels when something is being pointed toward me. It’s like they’re warning me to be careful about something in my life, even if they won’t come right out and say it plain.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are unique to you, or could they also apply to someone else in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: I suppose… well, doctor, I suppose words can apply to anybody if you look at them one way. Like if a radio man says, “Be careful out there,” I know anybody listening could hear that. I’m not saying the whole world stops for me or anything like that.\n\nBut the way it happens with me, it feels more particular than that. The men at the domino table will say something small, maybe nothing much to someone else, but it comes right after something I’ve been worrying on, or they say it in a tone that makes me think, now that was meant for me. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.\n\nMy wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe you're misinterpreting these messages, or that they might not be meant for you after all?\n\nPatient: Well… I’ve tried to tell myself that, doctor. I have. Especially when my wife says, “You’re making too much of it, it’s just talk,” I’ll sit there and think, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m hearing it wrong. I don’t want to be a foolish old man seeing things where there’s nothing.\n\nBut then it happens again. The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think these messages are something that you can't change your mind about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well… I don’t know if I’d say no matter what anyone says, doctor, because I do listen. I listen to my wife, and I listen to you sitting here. I’m not trying to be hardheaded just for the sake of it.

But the truth is, even when my wife tells me, “That’s just regular talk,” I still know how it feels to me. The men at the domino table, the way they say little things, and then sometimes the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it… it feels like messages meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.

So I can try to think different, and I can tell myself maybe I’m making too much of it, but it doesn’t really leave me. It stays in my mind. It feels too pointed, too lined up, to just be nothing. That’s why I came in, because even with folks telling me it’s only talk, I still feel like those messages are for me.

when the house is quiet, I hear my old friend call my name from the porch or hum a choir song, clear like he is standing right there.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: At domino games I have started tapping the tiles in the same pattern over and over and holding my hand up like I am stopping traffic, even though the others tell me to just play.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: I still love my family, but many days I just sit at the kitchen table, say very little, and let the garden and choir practice go by without feeling like moving.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: There are weeks when I wake up feeling so bright and important that I laugh loud and talk fast, but if someone slows me down or questions me, I get sharp with them.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: When that energy comes, I start cleaning the yard, fixing a chair, writing plans for church, and moving things around the house all in the same morning.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I get sure that I am the one who can bring the whole neighborhood together again, like I have a special gift for making people listen and follow my plans.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: On those nights I may sleep only two or three hours, then get up before daylight feeling ready to work like I had a full rest.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My mind runs from the choir to baseball to repairs to my friend and back again so fast that I cannot hold one thought long enough to explain it right.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: If I am trying to read the church paper or talk with my wife, a car outside, a bird, or a sound from the radio pulls me away and I forget what I was doing.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During my bright, restless weeks when I am sleeping little and making big plans, I still hear my friend’s voice and feel the radio or domino men are sending me warnings.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: Since all this started, more months have been taken up by those high-energy spells and their aftereffects than by my calmer stretches.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a quiet month when I was sleeping normally and not full of big plans, but I still heard my friend on the porch and go, and made the domino games uncomfortable enough that I do not join like I used to.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on and special messages have been happening on and off for about 9 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: Those weeks of high energy, little sleep, and big plans have been coming back for about 11 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 84 / not_in_workforce / less_than_9th\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are unique to you, or could they also apply to someone else in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: I suppose… well, doctor, I suppose words can apply to anybody if you look at them one way. Like if a radio man says, “Be careful out there,” I know anybody listening could hear that. I’m not saying the whole world stops for me or anything like that.\n\nBut the way it happens with me, it feels more particular than that. The men at the domino table will say something small, maybe nothing much to someone else, but it comes right after something I’ve been worrying on, or they say it in a tone that makes me think, now that was meant for me. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.\n\nMy wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe you're misinterpreting these messages, or that they might not be meant for you after all?\n\nPatient: Well… I’ve tried to tell myself that, doctor. I have. Especially when my wife says, “You’re making too much of it, it’s just talk,” I’ll sit there and think, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m hearing it wrong. I don’t want to be a foolish old man seeing things where there’s nothing.\n\nBut then it happens again. The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are something that you can't change your mind about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d say no matter what anyone says, doctor, because I do listen. I listen to my wife, and I listen to you sitting here. I’m not trying to be hardheaded just for the sake of it.\n\nBut the truth is, even when my wife tells me, “That’s just regular talk,” I still know how it feels to me. The men at the domino table, the way they say little things, and then sometimes the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it… it feels like messages meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo I can try to think different, and I can tell myself maybe I’m making too much of it, but it doesn’t really leave me. It stays in my mind. It feels too pointed, too lined up, to just be nothing. That’s why I came in, because even with folks telling me it’s only talk, I still feel like those messages are for me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think you could ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I don’t know, doctor… I’d like to say yes, because it tires me out thinking on it. I don’t enjoy sitting there wondering if every little thing is a warning. It feels too much like they’re telling me to be careful. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe if I could be shown somehow, or if it stopped happening, maybe… but right now, no, I don’t feel like I can just put it down. It still feels like those messages are for me.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I came in because there’s been some things happening that my wife says I’m making too much of, but I don’t think so. When I sit with the men at the domino table, they’ll say certain things—not directly to me, you understand, but the way they say it, the timing of it, it feels like they’re giving me little messages. Like warnings, mostly. And sometimes even the radio announcer, just talking like normal, but there’ll be a phrase that lands just so, and I know it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife tells me, “That’s just talk, don’t pay it any mind,” but I can’t shake it. I know when someone is trying to tell me to be careful. I’m old, but I’m not foolish. So I figured I better come tell somebody before it gets… well, before I keep worrying on it by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have these messages been affecting your daily life or activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir… I’d have to say it has, though I don’t like admitting that. I used to be regular with the church choir rehearsals, you know, even if my voice isn’t what it used to be. But I’ve missed some because I get to thinking maybe folks are saying things around me, or I don’t feel settled enough to go sit there with everybody.\n\nAnd my garden too—now that bothers me. I’ve always kept after it, even if it’s just a little patch. But some parts have gotten away from me lately, weeds and all, because I’ll go out there and start thinking on what I heard, turning it over and over, and then I don’t finish what I meant to do.\n\nThe domino games… well, that’s maybe the biggest one socially. I used to sit with the men and play, talk a little, laugh some. But I guess I’ve made it uncomfortable, asking what they meant or getting quiet when I think something is aimed my way. So I don’t join in like I used to. I still want to, but it doesn’t feel easy anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are part of a larger plan or scheme against you?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, doctor. I don’t want to sound like I’m saying everybody’s in on something, because that’s not quite how I mean it. But it does feel connected somehow, like the things I hear at the domino table and then later on the radio, they’re not just random talk.\n\nThe men will say something in a certain way, or right after I’ve been thinking about something, and it feels like they’re giving me a warning without saying it straight out. Then the radio announcer might use a phrase that lands the same way, and I think, well, there it is again. My wife says it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe she wants to calm me down, but I know when something is meant for me.\n\nSo… a plan? I’m not sure. But I do believe somebody, somehow, is trying to warn me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are specifically about you or something that's happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir, I do think they’re about me. My wife tells me it’s only regular talk, and maybe she means well, but I know how it feels when something is being pointed toward me. It’s like they’re warning me to be careful about something in my life, even if they won’t come right out and say it plain.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are unique to you, or could they also apply to someone else in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: I suppose… well, doctor, I suppose words can apply to anybody if you look at them one way. I’m not saying the whole world stops for me or anything like that.\n\nBut the way it happens with me, it feels more particular than that. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.\n\nMy wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe you're misinterpreting these messages, or that they might not be meant for you after all?\n\nPatient: Well… I’ve tried to tell myself that, doctor. I have. Especially when my wife says, “You’re making too much of it, it’s just talk,” I’ll sit there and think, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m hearing it wrong. I don’t want to be a foolish old man seeing things where there’s nothing.\n\nBut then it happens again. The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are something that you can't change your mind about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d say no matter what anyone says, doctor, because I do listen. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.\n\nMy wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe you're misinterpreting these messages, or that they might not be meant for you after all?\n\nPatient: Well… I’ve tried to tell myself that, doctor. I have. Especially when my wife says, “You’re making too much of it, it’s just talk,” I’ll sit there and think, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m hearing it wrong. I don’t want to be a foolish old man seeing things where there’s nothing.\n\nBut then it happens again. The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are something that you can't change your mind about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d say no matter what anyone says, doctor, because I do listen. I listen to my wife, and I listen to you sitting here. I’m not trying to be hardheaded just for the sake of it.\n\nBut the truth is, even when my wife tells me, “That’s just regular talk,” I still know how it feels to me. The men at the domino table, the way they say little things, and then sometimes the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it… it feels like messages meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo I can try to think different, and I can tell myself maybe I’m making too much of it, but it doesn’t really leave me. It stays in my mind. It feels too pointed, too lined up, to just be nothing. That’s why I came in, because even with folks telling me it’s only talk, I still feel like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor… I’d like to say yes, because it tires me out thinking on it. I don’t enjoy sitting there wondering if every little thing is a warning. I came here because of that, because the men at the domino table will say something small, and then maybe later the radio announcer says something that seems to fit right with it, and it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife, she’ll tell me, “That’s just talk,” and I do hear her. I’m not trying to argue with her all the time. But even when I try to let it go, it sits in my mind. It feels too much like they’re telling me to be careful. So could I ever stop believing it? There’s… maybe a little possibility, doctor. I mean, I’m sitting here with you because part of me knows my wife might be right when she says, “That’s just talk, don’t make it into something.” I do try to tell myself that. I’ll sit there after the domino game and think, well, maybe those men weren’t talking about me at all, maybe I just took it that way.

But then the thing is, it happens in such a way that it doesn’t feel like plain talk to me. The men at the domino table will say something small, something that sounds ordinary to somebody else, but it comes right when I’m thinking something, or it lines up with what I’ve been worrying about. And then sometimes the radio announcer says a little thing later that seems to match it, like it’s connected. That’s when I get that feeling in my chest like, no, this is meant for me.

So yes, I can say there’s a possibility it’s my interpretation… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is. I came because those little messages feel meant for me, and even when my wife tells me it’s nothing, I know they’re warning me to be careful.

It still feels like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that these messages are just your interpretation and not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: There’s… maybe a little possibility, doctor. Completely stop? I don’t know, doctor… that’s a hard thing for me to say. I’d like to be able to say, “Yes, if somebody explained it just right, I’d let it go,” because I don’t want to keep carrying it around in my head. My wife, she tries. She’ll tell me, “That’s just talk, they’re not talking to you,” and I do hear her words. I’m not deaf to it. I sit with it and I try to reason it out.

But then when I’m at the domino table and the men say some little thing — maybe it sounds ordinary to everybody else — it comes across to me like it’s got another meaning. Like it’s aimed my way. And then sometimes later the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it, and that’s when I feel sure again, like, see, that was a message. Like they’re warning me to be careful.

So could someone say something that would make me completely stop believing it? Right now, I don’t think so. Not completely. Maybe I could doubt it for a little while, maybe I could try to tell myself my wife is right, but it doesn’t go away all the way. It feels too pointed to me, too much like it’s meant for me.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I came in because there’s been some things happening that my wife says I’m making too much of, but I don’t think so. When I sit with the men at the domino table, they’ll say certain things—not directly to me, you understand, but the way they say it, the timing of it, it feels like they’re giving me little messages. Like warnings, mostly. And sometimes even the radio announcer, just talking like normal, but there’ll be a phrase that lands just so, and I know it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife tells me, “That’s just talk, don’t pay it any mind,” but I can’t shake it. I know when someone is trying to tell me to be careful. I’m old, but I’m not foolish. So I figured I better come tell somebody before it gets… well, before I keep worrying on it by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have these messages been affecting your daily life or activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir… I’d have to say it has, though I don’t like admitting that. I used to be regular with the church choir rehearsals, you know, even if my voice isn’t what it used to be. But I’ve missed some because I get to thinking maybe folks are saying things around me, or I don’t feel settled enough to go sit there with everybody.\n\nAnd my garden too—now that bothers me. I’ve always kept after it, even if it’s just a little patch. But some parts have gotten away from me lately, weeds and all, because I’ll go out there and start thinking on what I heard, turning it over and over, and then I don’t finish what I meant to do.\n\nThe domino games… well, that’s maybe the biggest one socially. I used to sit with the men and play, talk a little, laugh some. But I guess I’ve made it uncomfortable, asking what they meant or getting quiet when I think something is aimed my way. So I don’t join in like I used to. I still want to, but it doesn’t feel easy anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are part of a larger plan or scheme against you?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, doctor. I don’t want to sound like I’m saying everybody’s in on something, because that’s not quite how I mean it. But it does feel connected somehow, like the things I hear at the domino table and then later on the radio, they’re not just random talk.\n\nThe men will say something in a certain way, or right after I’ve been thinking about something, and it feels like they’re giving me a warning without saying it straight out. Then the radio announcer might use a phrase that lands the same way, and I think, well, there it is again. My wife says it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe she wants to calm me down, but I know when something is meant for me.\n\nSo… a plan? I’m not sure. But I do believe somebody, somehow, is trying to warn me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are specifically about you or something that's happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir, I do think they’re about me. Not every single word folks say, I’m not saying that, but certain things… the way the men at the domino table say it, or the timing of it, it feels like it’s meant for my ears.\n\nAnd then when the radio announcer says something that fits with it, I can’t just brush that off. My wife tells me it’s only regular talk, and maybe she means well, but I know how it feels when something is being pointed toward me. It’s like they’re warning me to be careful about something in my life, even if they won’t come right out and say it plain.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are unique to you, or could they also apply to someone else in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: I suppose… well, doctor, I suppose words can apply to anybody if you look at them one way. Like if a radio man says, “Be careful out there,” I know anybody listening could hear that. I’m not saying the whole world stops for me or anything like that.\n\nBut the way it happens with me, it feels more particular than that. The men at the domino table will say something small, maybe nothing much to someone else, but it comes right after something I’ve been worrying on, or they say it in a tone that makes me think, now that was meant for me. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.\n\nMy wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe you're misinterpreting these messages, or that they might not be meant for you after all?\n\nPatient: Well… I’ve tried to tell myself that, doctor. I have. Especially when my wife says, “You’re making too much of it, it’s just talk,” I’ll sit there and think, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m hearing it wrong. I don’t want to be a foolish old man seeing things where there’s nothing.\n\nBut then it happens again. The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are something that you can't change your mind about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d say no matter what anyone says, doctor, because I do listen. I listen to my wife, and I listen to you sitting here. I’m not trying to be hardheaded just for the sake of it.\n\nBut the truth is, even when my wife tells me, “That’s just regular talk,” I still know how it feels to me. The men at the domino table, the way they say little things, and then sometimes the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it… it feels like messages meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo I can try to think different, and I can tell myself maybe I’m making too much of it, but it doesn’t really leave me. It stays in my mind. It feels too pointed, too lined up, to just be nothing. That’s why I came in, because even with folks telling me it’s only talk, I still feel like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor… I’d like to say yes, because it tires me out thinking on it. I don’t enjoy sitting there wondering if every little thing is a warning. I came here because of that, because the men at the domino table will say something small, and then maybe later the radio announcer says something that seems to fit right with it, and it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife, she’ll tell me, “That’s just talk,” and I do hear her. I’m not trying to argue with her all the time. But even when I try to let it go, it sits in my mind. It feels too much like they’re telling me to be careful. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe if I could be shown somehow, or if it stopped happening, maybe… but right now, no, I don’t feel like I can just put it down. It still feels like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that these messages are just your interpretation and not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: There’s… maybe a little possibility, doctor. I mean, I’m sitting here with you because part of me knows my wife might be right when she says, “That’s just talk, don’t make it into something.” I do try to tell myself that. I’ll sit there after the domino game and think, well, maybe those men weren’t talking about me at all, maybe I just took it that way.\n\nBut then the thing is, it happens in such a way that it doesn’t feel like plain talk to me. The men at the domino table will say something small, something that sounds ordinary to somebody else, but it comes right when I’m thinking something, or it lines up with what I’ve been worrying about. And then sometimes the radio announcer says a little thing later that seems to match it, like it’s connected. That’s when I get that feeling in my chest like, no, this is meant for me.\n\nSo yes, I can say there’s a possibility it’s my interpretation… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is. I came because those little messages feel meant for me, and even when my wife tells me it’s nothing, I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or anything someone could say that would make you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Completely stop? I don’t know, doctor… that’s a hard thing for me to say. I’d like to be able to say, “Yes, if somebody explained it just right, I’d let it go,” because I don’t want to keep carrying it around in my head. My wife, she tries. She’ll tell me, “That’s just talk, they’re not talking to you,” and I do hear her words. I’m not deaf to it. I sit with it and I try to reason it out.\n\nBut then when I’m at the domino table and the men say some little thing — maybe it sounds ordinary to everybody else — it comes across to me like it’s got another meaning. Like it’s aimed my way. And then sometimes later the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it, and that’s when I feel sure again, like, see, that was a message. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo could someone say something that would make me completely stop believing it? Right now, I don’t think so. Not completely. Maybe I could doubt it for a little while, maybe I could try to tell myself my wife is right, but it doesn’t go away all the way. It feels too pointed to me, too much like it’s meant for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

And then sometimes the radio announcer says a little thing later that seems to match it, like it’s connected. That’s when I get that feeling in my chest like, no, this is meant for me.\n\nSo yes, I can say there’s a possibility it’s my interpretation… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is. I came because those little messages feel meant for me, and even when my wife tells me it’s nothing, I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or anything someone could say that would make you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Completely stop? I don’t know, doctor… that’s a hard thing for me to say. I’d like to be able to say, “Yes, if somebody explained it just right, I’d let it go,” because I don’t want to keep carrying it around in my head. My wife, she tries. She’ll tell me, “That’s just talk, they’re not talking to you,” and I do hear her words. I’m not deaf to it. I sit with it and I try to reason it out.\n\nBut then when I’m at the domino table and the men say some little thing — maybe it sounds ordinary to everybody else — it comes across to me like it’s got another meaning. Like it’s aimed my way. And then sometimes later the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it, and that’s when I feel sure again, like, see, that was a message. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo could someone say something that would make me completely stop believing it? Right now, I don’t think so. Not completely. Maybe I could doubt it for a little while, maybe I could try to tell myself my wife is right, but it doesn’t go away all the way. It feels too pointed to me, too much like it’s meant for me.\n\n[Last Question]\nIf someone you trust, like a close family member or a respected community leader, told you that they had talked to the men at the domino table and the radio announcer, and they all said they were not trying to send you messages, would that change your mind about what you're hearing?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It might make me stop and think for a bit, doctor. If it was someone I trust, I wouldn’t just throw their words away. I’d listen, because I don’t want to be stubborn about it, and I know my wife already tells me, “That’s just talk.” So if a family member or somebody respected came and said they talked to those men and even the radio announcer, and they said, “No, nobody is sending you messages,” I’d hear that.

But I have to be honest with you… I don’t know that it would change my mind all the way. Because when I’m there at the domino table, and the men say those little things, it feels like it’s meant for me. It’s not always big words, you know, it’s small things, but the timing of it, the way it comes when I’m already thinking or worrying, it feels pointed. And then sometimes the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, and that makes it hard for me to say it’s nothing.

So maybe I would doubt myself for a little while if someone I trust told me that. I might try to tell myself, “Okay, maybe they’re right, maybe it’s just talk.” But deep down, doctor, I think I’d still feel like those messages are for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I came in because there’s been some things happening that my wife says I’m making too much of, but I don’t think so. When I sit with the men at the domino table, they’ll say certain things—not directly to me, you understand, but the way they say it, the timing of it, it feels like they’re giving me little messages. Like warnings, mostly. And sometimes even the radio announcer, just talking like normal, but there’ll be a phrase that lands just so, and I know it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife tells me, “That’s just talk, don’t pay it any mind,” but I can’t shake it. I know when someone is trying to tell me to be careful. I’m old, but I’m not foolish. So I figured I better come tell somebody before it gets… well, before I keep worrying on it by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have these messages been affecting your daily life or activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir… I’d have to say it has, though I don’t like admitting that. I used to be regular with the church choir rehearsals, you know, even if my voice isn’t what it used to be. But I’ve missed some because I get to thinking maybe folks are saying things around me, or I don’t feel settled enough to go sit there with everybody.\n\nAnd my garden too—now that bothers me. I’ve always kept after it, even if it’s just a little patch. But some parts have gotten away from me lately, weeds and all, because I’ll go out there and start thinking on what I heard, turning it over and over, and then I don’t finish what I meant to do.\n\nThe domino games… well, that’s maybe the biggest one socially. I used to sit with the men and play, talk a little, laugh some. But I guess I’ve made it uncomfortable, asking what they meant or getting quiet when I think something is aimed my way. So I don’t join in like I used to. I still want to, but it doesn’t feel easy anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are part of a larger plan or scheme against you?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, doctor. I don’t want to sound like I’m saying everybody’s in on something, because that’s not quite how I mean it. But it does feel connected somehow, like the things I hear at the domino table and then later on the radio, they’re not just random talk.\n\nThe men will say something in a certain way, or right after I’ve been thinking about something, and it feels like they’re giving me a warning without saying it straight out. Then the radio announcer might use a phrase that lands the same way, and I think, well, there it is again. My wife says it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe she wants to calm me down, but I know when something is meant for me.\n\nSo… a plan? I’m not sure. But I do believe somebody, somehow, is trying to warn me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are specifically about you or something that's happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir, I do think they’re about me. Not every single word folks say, I’m not saying that, but certain things… the way the men at the domino table say it, or the timing of it, it feels like it’s meant for my ears.\n\nAnd then when the radio announcer says something that fits with it, I can’t just brush that off. My wife tells me it’s only regular talk, and maybe she means well, but I know how it feels when something is being pointed toward me. It’s like they’re warning me to be careful about something in my life, even if they won’t come right out and say it plain.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are unique to you, or could they also apply to someone else in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: I suppose… well, doctor, I suppose words can apply to anybody if you look at them one way. Like if a radio man says, “Be careful out there,” I know anybody listening could hear that. I’m not saying the whole world stops for me or anything like that.\n\nBut the way it happens with me, it feels more particular than that. The men at the domino table will say something small, maybe nothing much to someone else, but it comes right after something I’ve been worrying on, or they say it in a tone that makes me think, now that was meant for me. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.\n\nMy wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe you're misinterpreting these messages, or that they might not be meant for you after all?\n\nPatient: Well… I’ve tried to tell myself that, doctor. I have. Especially when my wife says, “You’re making too much of it, it’s just talk,” I’ll sit there and think, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m hearing it wrong. I don’t want to be a foolish old man seeing things where there’s nothing.\n\nBut then it happens again. The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are something that you can't change your mind about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d say no matter what anyone says, doctor, because I do listen. I listen to my wife, and I listen to you sitting here. I’m not trying to be hardheaded just for the sake of it.\n\nBut the truth is, even when my wife tells me, “That’s just regular talk,” I still know how it feels to me. The men at the domino table, the way they say little things, and then sometimes the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it… it feels like messages meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo I can try to think different, and I can tell myself maybe I’m making too much of it, but it doesn’t really leave me. It stays in my mind. It feels too pointed, too lined up, to just be nothing. That’s why I came in, because even with folks telling me it’s only talk, I still feel like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor… I’d like to say yes, because it tires me out thinking on it. I don’t enjoy sitting there wondering if every little thing is a warning. I came here because of that, because the men at the domino table will say something small, and then maybe later the radio announcer says something that seems to fit right with it, and it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife, she’ll tell me, “That’s just talk,” and I do hear her. I’m not trying to argue with her all the time. But even when I try to let it go, it sits in my mind. It feels too much like they’re telling me to be careful. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe if I could be shown somehow, or if it stopped happening, maybe… but right now, no, I don’t feel like I can just put it down. It still feels like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that these messages are just your interpretation and not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: There’s… maybe a little possibility, doctor. I mean, I’m sitting here with you because part of me knows my wife might be right when she says, “That’s just talk, don’t make it into something.” I do try to tell myself that. I’ll sit there after the domino game and think, well, maybe those men weren’t talking about me at all, maybe I just took it that way.\n\nBut then the thing is, it happens in such a way that it doesn’t feel like plain talk to me. The men at the domino table will say something small, something that sounds ordinary to somebody else, but it comes right when I’m thinking something, or it lines up with what I’ve been worrying about. And then sometimes the radio announcer says a little thing later that seems to match it, like it’s connected. That’s when I get that feeling in my chest like, no, this is meant for me.\n\nSo yes, I can say there’s a possibility it’s my interpretation… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is. I came because those little messages feel meant for me, and even when my wife tells me it’s nothing, I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or anything someone could say that would make you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Completely stop? I don’t know, doctor… that’s a hard thing for me to say. I’d like to be able to say, “Yes, if somebody explained it just right, I’d let it go,” because I don’t want to keep carrying it around in my head. My wife, she tries. She’ll tell me, “That’s just talk, they’re not talking to you,” and I do hear her words. I’m not deaf to it. I sit with it and I try to reason it out.\n\nBut then when I’m at the domino table and the men say some little thing — maybe it sounds ordinary to everybody else — it comes across to me like it’s got another meaning. Like it’s aimed my way. And then sometimes later the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it, and that’s when I feel sure again, like, see, that was a message. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo could someone say something that would make me completely stop believing it? Right now, I don’t think so. Not completely. Maybe I could doubt it for a little while, maybe I could try to tell myself my wife is right, but it doesn’t go away all the way. It feels too pointed to me, too much like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trust, like a close family member or a respected community leader, told you that they had talked to the men at the domino table and the radio announcer, and they all said they were not trying to send you messages, would that change your mind about what you're hearing?\n\nPatient: It might make me stop and think for a bit, doctor. If it was someone I trust, I wouldn’t just throw their words away. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are something that you can't change your mind about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d say no matter what anyone says, doctor, because I do listen. I listen to my wife, and I listen to you sitting here. I’m not trying to be hardheaded just for the sake of it.\n\nBut the truth is, even when my wife tells me, “That’s just regular talk,” I still know how it feels to me. The men at the domino table, the way they say little things, and then sometimes the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it… it feels like messages meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo I can try to think different, and I can tell myself maybe I’m making too much of it, but it doesn’t really leave me. It stays in my mind. It feels too pointed, too lined up, to just be nothing. That’s why I came in, because even with folks telling me it’s only talk, I still feel like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor… I’d like to say yes, because it tires me out thinking on it. I don’t enjoy sitting there wondering if every little thing is a warning. I came here because of that, because the men at the domino table will say something small, and then maybe later the radio announcer says something that seems to fit right with it, and it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife, she’ll tell me, “That’s just talk,” and I do hear her. I’m not trying to argue with her all the time. But even when I try to let it go, it sits in my mind. It feels too much like they’re telling me to be careful. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe if I could be shown somehow, or if it stopped happening, maybe… but right now, no, I don’t feel like I can just put it down. It still feels like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that these messages are just your interpretation and not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: There’s… maybe a little possibility, doctor. I mean, I’m sitting here with you because part of me knows my wife might be right when she says, “That’s just talk, don’t make it into something.” I do try to tell myself that. I’ll sit there after the domino game and think, well, maybe those men weren’t talking about me at all, maybe I just took it that way.\n\nBut then the thing is, it happens in such a way that it doesn’t feel like plain talk to me. The men at the domino table will say something small, something that sounds ordinary to somebody else, but it comes right when I’m thinking something, or it lines up with what I’ve been worrying about. And then sometimes the radio announcer says a little thing later that seems to match it, like it’s connected. That’s when I get that feeling in my chest like, no, this is meant for me.\n\nSo yes, I can say there’s a possibility it’s my interpretation… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is. I came because those little messages feel meant for me, and even when my wife tells me it’s nothing, I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or anything someone could say that would make you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Completely stop? I don’t know, doctor… that’s a hard thing for me to say. I’d like to be able to say, “Yes, if somebody explained it just right, I’d let it go,” because I don’t want to keep carrying it around in my head. My wife, she tries. She’ll tell me, “That’s just talk, they’re not talking to you,” and I do hear her words. I’m not deaf to it. If it was someone I trust, I wouldn’t just throw their words away. There are times I’m not thinking about it every minute, doctor, yes. Like if I’m busy with something else or sitting with my wife and things are quiet, I can go a while without it being right in the front of my mind. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m hearing warnings every second of the day.

If I go by the domino table, or I hear the men talking, and one of them says some little thing that fits too close with what I’ve been thinking, then right away I feel it again. Or if the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, then I think, there it is, that’s for me.

So yes, I’ve had stretches where I’m not focused on it, but I still came here because those messages keep coming back in that way. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to let that sit in my head, but when it happens, I know it feels meant for me. It feels like they’re warning me to be careful.

Then the radio announcer might use a phrase that lands the same way, and I think, well, there it is again. My wife says it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe she wants to calm me down, but I know when something is meant for me.\n\nSo… a plan? I’m not sure. But I do believe somebody, somehow, is trying to warn me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are specifically about you or something that's happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir, I do think they’re about me. Not every single word folks say, I’m not saying that, but certain things… the way the men at the domino table say it, or the timing of it, it feels like it’s meant for my ears.\n\nAnd then when the radio announcer says something that fits with it, I can’t just brush that off. My wife tells me it’s only regular talk, and maybe she means well, but I know how it feels when something is being pointed toward me. It’s like they’re warning me to be careful about something in my life, even if they won’t come right out and say it plain.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are unique to you, or could they also apply to someone else in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: I suppose… well, doctor, I suppose words can apply to anybody if you look at them one way. Like if a radio man says, “Be careful out there,” I know anybody listening could hear that. I’m not saying the whole world stops for me or anything like that.\n\nBut the way it happens with me, it feels more particular than that. The men at the domino table will say something small, maybe nothing much to someone else, but it comes right after something I’ve been worrying on, or they say it in a tone that makes me think, now that was meant for me. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.\n\nMy wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe you're misinterpreting these messages, or that they might not be meant for you after all?\n\nPatient: Well… I’ve tried to tell myself that, doctor. I have. Especially when my wife says, “You’re making too much of it, it’s just talk,” I’ll sit there and think, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m hearing it wrong. I don’t want to be a foolish old man seeing things where there’s nothing.\n\nBut then it happens again. The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are something that you can't change your mind about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d say no matter what anyone says, doctor, because I do listen. I listen to my wife, and I listen to you sitting here. I’m not trying to be hardheaded just for the sake of it.\n\nBut the truth is, even when my wife tells me, “That’s just regular talk,” I still know how it feels to me. The men at the domino table, the way they say little things, and then sometimes the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it… it feels like messages meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo I can try to think different, and I can tell myself maybe I’m making too much of it, but it doesn’t really leave me. It stays in my mind. It feels too pointed, too lined up, to just be nothing. That’s why I came in, because even with folks telling me it’s only talk, I still feel like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor… I’d like to say yes, because it tires me out thinking on it. I don’t enjoy sitting there wondering if every little thing is a warning. I came here because of that, because the men at the domino table will say something small, and then maybe later the radio announcer says something that seems to fit right with it, and it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife, she’ll tell me, “That’s just talk,” and I do hear her. I’m not trying to argue with her all the time. But even when I try to let it go, it sits in my mind. It feels too much like they’re telling me to be careful. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe if I could be shown somehow, or if it stopped happening, maybe… but right now, no, I don’t feel like I can just put it down. It still feels like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that these messages are just your interpretation and not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: There’s… maybe a little possibility, doctor. I mean, I’m sitting here with you because part of me knows my wife might be right when she says, “That’s just talk, don’t make it into something.” I do try to tell myself that. I’ll sit there after the domino game and think, well, maybe those men weren’t talking about me at all, maybe I just took it that way.\n\nBut then the thing is, it happens in such a way that it doesn’t feel like plain talk to me. The men at the domino table will say something small, something that sounds ordinary to somebody else, but it comes right when I’m thinking something, or it lines up with what I’ve been worrying about. And then sometimes the radio announcer says a little thing later that seems to match it, like it’s connected. That’s when I get that feeling in my chest like, no, this is meant for me.\n\nSo yes, I can say there’s a possibility it’s my interpretation… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is. I came because those little messages feel meant for me, and even when my wife tells me it’s nothing, I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or anything someone could say that would make you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Completely stop? I don’t know, doctor… that’s a hard thing for me to say. I’d like to be able to say, “Yes, if somebody explained it just right, I’d let it go,” because I don’t want to keep carrying it around in my head. My wife, she tries. She’ll tell me, “That’s just talk, they’re not talking to you,” and I do hear her words. I’m not deaf to it. I sit with it and I try to reason it out.\n\nBut then when I’m at the domino table and the men say some little thing — maybe it sounds ordinary to everybody else — it comes across to me like it’s got another meaning. Like it’s aimed my way. And then sometimes later the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it, and that’s when I feel sure again, like, see, that was a message. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo could someone say something that would make me completely stop believing it? Right now, I don’t think so. Not completely. Maybe I could doubt it for a little while, maybe I could try to tell myself my wife is right, but it doesn’t go away all the way. It feels too pointed to me, too much like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trust, like a close family member or a respected community leader, told you that they had talked to the men at the domino table and the radio announcer, and they all said they were not trying to send you messages, would that change your mind about what you're hearing?\n\nPatient: It might make me stop and think for a bit, doctor. If it was someone I trust, I wouldn’t just throw their words away. I’d listen, because I don’t want to be stubborn about it, and I know my wife already tells me, “That’s just talk.” So if a family member or somebody respected came and said they talked to those men and even the radio announcer, and they said, “No, nobody is sending you messages,” I’d hear that.\n\nBut I have to be honest with you… I don’t know that it would change my mind all the way. Because when I’m there at the domino table, and the men say those little things, it feels like it’s meant for me. It’s not always big words, you know, it’s small things, but the timing of it, the way it comes when I’m already thinking or worrying, it feels pointed. And then sometimes the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, and that makes it hard for me to say it’s nothing.\n\nSo maybe I would doubt myself for a little while if someone I trust told me that. I might try to tell myself, “Okay, maybe they’re right, maybe it’s just talk.” But deep down, doctor, I think I’d still feel like those messages are for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think about these messages or feel like you were receiving warnings?\n\nPatient: There are times I’m not thinking about it every minute, doctor, yes. Like if I’m busy with something else or sitting with my wife and things are quiet, I can go a while without it being right in the front of my mind. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m hearing warnings every second of the day.\n\nBut it doesn’t really leave me completely. It’s more like… it gets quiet, and then something brings it back. If I go by the domino table, or I hear the men talking, and one of them says some little thing that fits too close with what I’ve been thinking, then right away I feel it again. Or if the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, then I think, there it is, that’s for me.\n\nSo yes, I’ve had stretches where I’m not focused on it, but I still came here because those messages keep coming back in that way. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to let that sit in my head, but when it happens, I know it feels meant for me. It feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
