high energy, little sleep, and big plans have been coming back for about 11 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 84 / not_in_workforce / less_than_9th\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or anything someone could say that would make you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Completely stop? I don’t know, doctor… that’s a hard thing for me to say. I’d like to be able to say, “Yes, if somebody explained it just right, I’d let it go,” because I don’t want to keep carrying it around in my head. My wife, she tries. She’ll tell me, “That’s just talk, they’re not talking to you,” and I do hear her words. I’m not deaf to it. I sit with it and I try to reason it out.\n\nBut then when I’m at the domino table and the men say some little thing — maybe it sounds ordinary to everybody else — it comes across to me like it’s got another meaning. Like it’s aimed my way. And then sometimes later the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it, and that’s when I feel sure again, like, see, that was a message. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo could someone say something that would make me completely stop believing it? Right now, I don’t think so. Not completely. Maybe I could doubt it for a little while, maybe I could try to tell myself my wife is right, but it doesn’t go away all the way. It feels too pointed to me, too much like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trust, like a close family member or a respected community leader, told you that they had talked to the men at the domino table and the radio announcer, and they all said they were not trying to send you messages, would that change your mind about what you're hearing?\n\nPatient: It might make me stop and think for a bit, doctor. If it was someone I trust, I wouldn’t just throw their words away. I’d listen, because I don’t want to be stubborn about it, and I know my wife already tells me, “That’s just talk.” So if a family member or somebody respected came and said they talked to those men and even the radio announcer, and they said, “No, nobody is sending you messages,” I’d hear that.\n\nBut I have to be honest with you… I don’t know that it would change my mind all the way. Because when I’m there at the domino table, and the men say those little things, it feels like it’s meant for me. It’s not always big words, you know, it’s small things, but the timing of it, the way it comes when I’m already thinking or worrying, it feels pointed. And then sometimes the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, and that makes it hard for me to say it’s nothing.\n\nSo maybe I would doubt myself for a little while if someone I trust told me that. I might try to tell myself, “Okay, maybe they’re right, maybe it’s just talk.” But deep down, doctor, I think I’d still feel like those messages are for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think about these messages or feel like you were receiving warnings?\n\nPatient: There are times I’m not thinking about it every minute, doctor, yes. Like if I’m busy with something else or sitting with my wife and things are quiet, I can go a while without it being right in the front of my mind. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m hearing warnings every second of the day.\n\nBut it doesn’t really leave me completely. It’s more like… it gets quiet, and then something brings it back. If I go by the domino table, or I hear the men talking, and one of them says some little thing that fits too close with what I’ve been thinking, then right away I feel it again. Or if the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, then I think, there it is, that’s for me.\n\nSo yes, I’ve had stretches where I’m not focused on it, but I still came here because those messages keep coming back in that way. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to let that sit in my head, but when it happens, I know it feels meant for me. It feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow long have you been feeling like you're receiving these messages?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I came in because there’s been some things happening that my wife says I’m making too much of, but I don’t think so. When I sit with the men at the domino table, they’ll say certain things—not directly to me, you understand, but the way they say it, the timing of it, it feels like they’re giving me little messages. Like warnings, mostly. And sometimes even the radio announcer, just talking like normal, but there’ll be a phrase that lands just so, and I know it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife tells me, “That’s just talk, don’t pay it any mind,” but I can’t shake it. I know when someone is trying to tell me to be careful. I’m old, but I’m not foolish. So I figured I better come tell somebody before it gets… well, before I keep worrying on it by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have these messages been affecting your daily life or activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir… I’d have to say it has, though I don’t like admitting that. I used to be regular with the church choir rehearsals, you know, even if my voice isn’t what it used to be. But I’ve missed some because I get to thinking maybe folks are saying things around me, or I don’t feel settled enough to go sit there with everybody.\n\nAnd my garden too—now that bothers me. I’ve always kept after it, even if it’s just a little patch. But some parts have gotten away from me lately, weeds and all, because I’ll go out there and start thinking on what I heard, turning it over and over, and then I don’t finish what I meant to do.\n\nThe domino games… well, that’s maybe the biggest one socially. I used to sit with the men and play, talk a little, laugh some. But I guess I’ve made it uncomfortable, asking what they meant or getting quiet when I think something is aimed my way. So I don’t join in like I used to. I still want to, but it doesn’t feel easy anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are part of a larger plan or scheme against you?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, doctor. I don’t want to sound like I’m saying everybody’s in on something, because that’s not quite how I mean it. But it does feel connected somehow, like the things I hear at the domino table and then later on the radio, they’re not just random talk.\n\nThe men will say something in a certain way, or right after I’ve been thinking about something, and it feels like they’re giving me a warning without saying it straight out. Then the radio announcer might use a phrase that lands the same way, and I think, well, there it is again. My wife says it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe she wants to calm me down, but I know when something is meant for me.\n\nSo… a plan? I’m not sure. But I do believe somebody, somehow, is trying to warn me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are specifically about you or something that's happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir, I do think they’re about me. Not every single word folks say, I’m not saying that, but certain things… the way the men at the domino table say it, or the timing of it, it feels like it’s meant for my ears.\n\nAnd then when the radio announcer says something that fits with it, I can’t just brush that off. My wife tells me it’s only regular talk, and maybe she means well, but I know how it feels when something is being pointed toward me. It’s like they’re warning me to be careful about something in my life, even if they won’t come right out and say it plain.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are unique to you, or could they also apply to someone else in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: I suppose… well, doctor, I suppose words can apply to anybody if you look at them one way. Like if a radio man says, “Be careful out there,” I know anybody listening could hear that. I’m not saying the whole world stops for me or anything like that.\n\nBut the way it happens with me, it feels more particular than that. The men at the domino table will say something small, maybe nothing much to someone else, but it comes right after something I’ve been worrying on, or they say it in a tone that makes me think, now that was meant for me. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.\n\nMy wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe you're misinterpreting these messages, or that they might not be meant for you after all?\n\nPatient: Well… I’ve tried to tell myself that, doctor. I have. Especially when my wife says, “You’re making too much of it, it’s just talk,” I’ll sit there and think, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m hearing it wrong. I don’t want to be a foolish old man seeing things where there’s nothing.\n\nBut then it happens again. The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are something that you can't change your mind about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d say no matter what anyone says, doctor, because I do listen. I listen to my wife, and I listen to you sitting here. I’m not trying to be hardheaded just for the sake of it.\n\nBut the truth is, even when my wife tells me, “That’s just regular talk,” I still know how it feels to me. The men at the domino table, the way they say little things, and then sometimes the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it… it feels like messages meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo I can try to think different, and I can tell myself maybe I’m making too much of it, but it doesn’t really leave me. It stays in my mind. It feels too pointed, too lined up, to just be nothing. That’s why I came in, because even with folks telling me it’s only talk, I still feel like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor… I’d like to say yes, because it tires me out thinking on it. I don’t enjoy sitting there wondering if every little thing is a warning. I came here because of that, because the men at the domino table will say something small, and then maybe later the radio announcer says something that seems to fit right with it, and it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife, she’ll tell me, “That’s just talk,” and I do hear her. I’m not trying to argue with her all the time. But even when I try to let it go, it sits in my mind. It feels too much like they’re telling me to be careful. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe if I could be shown somehow, or if it stopped happening, maybe… but right now, no, I don’t feel like I can just put it down. It still feels like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that these messages are just your interpretation and not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: There’s… maybe a little possibility, doctor. I mean, I’m sitting here with you because part of me knows my wife might be right when she says, “That’s just talk, don’t make it into something.” I do try to tell myself that. I’ll sit there after the domino game and think, well, maybe those men weren’t talking about me at all, maybe I just took it that way.\n\nBut then the thing is, it happens in such a way that it doesn’t feel like plain talk to me. The men at the domino table will say something small, something that sounds ordinary to somebody else, but it comes right when I’m thinking something, or it lines up with what I’ve been worrying about. And then sometimes the radio announcer says a little thing later that seems to match it, like it’s connected. That’s when I get that feeling in my chest like, no, this is meant for me.\n\nSo yes, I can say there’s a possibility it’s my interpretation… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is. I came because those little messages feel meant for me, and even when my wife tells me it’s nothing, I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or anything someone could say that would make you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Completely stop? I don’t know, doctor… that’s a hard thing for me to say. I used to sit with the men and play, talk a little, laugh some. But I guess I’ve made it uncomfortable, asking what they meant or getting quiet when I think something is aimed my way. So I don’t join in like I used to. I still want to, but it doesn’t feel easy anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are part of a larger plan or scheme against you?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, doctor. I don’t want to sound like I’m saying everybody’s in on something, because that’s not quite how I mean it. But it does feel connected somehow, like the things I hear at the domino table and then later on the radio, they’re not just random talk.\n\nThe men will say something in a certain way, or right after I’ve been thinking about something, and it feels like they’re giving me a warning without saying it straight out. Then the radio announcer might use a phrase that lands the same way, and I think, well, there it is again. My wife says it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe she wants to calm me down, but I know when something is meant for me.\n\nSo… a plan? I’m not sure. But I do believe somebody, somehow, is trying to warn me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are specifically about you or something that's happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir, I do think they’re about me. Not every single word folks say, I’m not saying that, but certain things… the way the men at the domino table say it, or the timing of it, it feels like it’s meant for my ears.\n\nAnd then when the radio announcer says something that fits with it, I can’t just brush that off. My wife tells me it’s only regular talk, and maybe she means well, but I know how it feels when something is being pointed toward me. It’s like they’re warning me to be careful about something in my life, even if they won’t come right out and say it plain.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are unique to you, or could they also apply to someone else in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: I suppose… well, doctor, I suppose words can apply to anybody if you look at them one way. Like if a radio man says, “Be careful out there,” I know anybody listening could hear that. I’m not saying the whole world stops for me or anything like that.\n\nBut the way it happens with me, it feels more particular than that. The men at the domino table will say something small, maybe nothing much to someone else, but it comes right after something I’ve been worrying on, or they say it in a tone that makes me think, now that was meant for me. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.\n\nMy wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe you're misinterpreting these messages, or that they might not be meant for you after all?\n\nPatient: Well… I’ve tried to tell myself that, doctor. I have. Especially when my wife says, “You’re making too much of it, it’s just talk,” I’ll sit there and think, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m hearing it wrong. I don’t want to be a foolish old man seeing things where there’s nothing.\n\nBut then it happens again. The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are something that you can't change your mind about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d say no matter what anyone says, doctor, because I do listen. I listen to my wife, and I listen to you sitting here. I’m not trying to be hardheaded just for the sake of it.\n\nBut the truth is, even when my wife tells me, “That’s just regular talk,” I still know how it feels to me. The men at the domino table, the way they say little things, and then sometimes the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it… it feels like messages meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo I can try to think different, and I can tell myself maybe I’m making too much of it, but it doesn’t really leave me. It stays in my mind. It feels too pointed, too lined up, to just be nothing. That’s why I came in, because even with folks telling me it’s only talk, I still feel like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor… I’d like to say yes, because it tires me out thinking on it. I don’t enjoy sitting there wondering if every little thing is a warning. I came here because of that, because the men at the domino table will say something small, and then maybe later the radio announcer says something that seems to fit right with it, and it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife, she’ll tell me, “That’s just talk,” and I do hear her. I’m not trying to argue with her all the time. But even when I try to let it go, it sits in my mind. It feels too much like they’re telling me to be careful. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe if I could be shown somehow, or if it stopped happening, maybe… but right now, no, I don’t feel like I can just put it down. It still feels like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that these messages are just your interpretation and not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: There’s… maybe a little possibility, doctor. I mean, I’m sitting here with you because part of me knows my wife might be right when she says, “That’s just talk, don’t make it into something.” I do try to tell myself that. I’ll sit there after the domino game and think, well, maybe those men weren’t talking about me at all, maybe I just took it that way.\n\nBut then the thing is, it happens in such a way that it doesn’t feel like plain talk to me. The men at the domino table will say something small, something that sounds ordinary to somebody else, but it comes right when I’m thinking something, or it lines up with what I’ve been worrying about. And then sometimes the radio announcer says a little thing later that seems to match it, like it’s connected. That’s when I get that feeling in my chest like, no, this is meant for me.\n\nSo yes, I can say there’s a possibility it’s my interpretation… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is. I came because those little messages feel meant for me, and even when my wife tells me it’s nothing, I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or anything someone could say that would make you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Completely stop? I don’t know, doctor… that’s a hard thing for me to say. I’d like to be able to say, “Yes, if somebody explained it just right, I’d let it go,” because I don’t want to keep carrying it around in my head. My wife, she tries. She’ll tell me, “That’s just talk, they’re not talking to you,” and I do hear her words. I’m not deaf to it. I sit with it and I try to reason it out.\n\nBut then when I’m at the domino table and the men say some little thing — maybe it sounds ordinary to everybody else — it comes across to me like it’s got another meaning. Like it’s aimed my way. And then sometimes later the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it, and that’s when I feel sure again, like, see, that was a message. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo could someone say something that would make me completely stop believing it? Right now, I don’t think so. Not completely. Maybe I could doubt it for a little while, maybe I could try to tell myself my wife is right, but it doesn’t go away all the way. It feels too pointed to me, too much like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trust, like a close family member or a respected community leader, told you that they had talked to the men at the domino table and the radio announcer, and they all said they were not trying to send you messages, would that change your mind about what you're hearing?\n\nPatient: It might make me stop and think for a bit, doctor. If it was someone I trust, I wouldn’t just throw their words away. I’d listen, because I don’t want to be stubborn about it, and I know my wife already tells me, “That’s just talk.” So if a family member or somebody respected came and said they talked to those men and even the radio announcer, and they said, “No, nobody is sending you messages,” I’d hear that.\n\nBut I have to be honest with you… I don’t know that it would change my mind all the way. Because when I’m there at the domino table, and the men say those little things, it feels like it’s meant for me. It’s not always big words, you know, it’s small things, but the timing of it, the way it comes when I’m already thinking or worrying, it feels pointed. And then sometimes the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, and that makes it hard for me to say it’s nothing.\n\nSo maybe I would doubt myself for a little while if someone I trust told me that. I might try to tell myself, “Okay, maybe they’re right, maybe it’s just talk.” But deep down, doctor, I think I’d still feel like those messages are for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think about these messages or feel like you were receiving warnings?\n\nPatient: There are times I’m not thinking about it every minute, doctor, yes. Like if I’m busy with something else or sitting with my wife and things are quiet, I can go a while without it being right in the front of my mind. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m hearing warnings every second of the day.\n\nBut it doesn’t really leave me completely. It’s more like… it gets quiet, and then something brings it back. If I go by the domino table, or I hear the men talking, and one of them says some little thing that fits too close with what I’ve been thinking, then right away I feel it again. Or if the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, then I think, there it is, that’s for me.\n\nSo yes, I’ve had stretches where I’m not focused on it, but I still came here because those messages keep coming back in that way. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to let that sit in my head, but when it happens, I know it feels meant for me. It feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you're receiving these messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about nine months now, doctor. Not every single day in the exact same way, but on and off for around nine months that I’ve been feeling like the voices and those special messages are coming through to me.\n\nAt first I didn’t know what to make of it, and I thought maybe I was just noticing things too much, but then it kept happening here and there. It would quiet down for a bit, and then something would come up again and I’d feel like, there it is, it’s meant for me. So yes… about nine months, on and off.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’ve tried to think about that, doctor, because my wife says to me, “That’s just talk, you’re making too much of it,” and I don’t want to argue with her all the time. I know she cares about me. So yes, I’ve asked myself, maybe it’s me, maybe I’m putting something on it.

But when I’m actually there, when those men at the domino table say certain little things, it doesn’t feel like it’s only in my own mind. It feels like it’s coming from them, like the words are meant for me. And sometimes when the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, that’s when it gets hard for me to believe it’s just my thoughts. It feels too pointed, too much like a warning.

So I can say maybe there’s a chance my mind is making it bigger, I’ll give you that. But deep down, I still feel those messages are being given to me from outside — from the men talking, or the radio — and that they’re telling me to be careful.

---------- turn 14 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct ----------
========== INPUT [doctor] ==========
[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I came in because there’s been some things happening that my wife says I’m making too much of, but I don’t think so. When I sit with the men at the domino table, they’ll say certain things—not directly to me, you understand, but the way they say it, the timing of it, it feels like they’re giving me little messages. Like warnings, mostly. And sometimes even the radio announcer, just talking like normal, but there’ll be a phrase that lands just so, and I know it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife tells me, “That’s just talk, don’t pay it any mind,” but I can’t shake it. I know when someone is trying to tell me to be careful. I’m old, but I’m not foolish. So I figured I better come tell somebody before it gets… well, before I keep worrying on it by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have these messages been affecting your daily life or activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir… I’d have to say it has, though I don’t like admitting that. I used to be regular with the church choir rehearsals, you know, even if my voice isn’t what it used to be. But I’ve missed some because I get to thinking maybe folks are saying things around me, or I don’t feel settled enough to go sit there with everybody.\n\nAnd my garden too—now that bothers me. I’ve always kept after it, even if it’s just a little patch. But some parts have gotten away from me lately, weeds and all, because I’ll go out there and start thinking on what I heard, turning it over and over, and then I don’t finish what I meant to do.\n\nThe domino games… well, that’s maybe the biggest one socially. I used to sit with the men and play, talk a little, laugh some. But I guess I’ve made it uncomfortable, asking what they meant or getting quiet when I think something is aimed my way. So I don’t join in like I used to. I still want to, but it doesn’t feel easy anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are part of a larger plan or scheme against you?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, doctor. I don’t want to sound like I’m saying everybody’s in on something, because that’s not quite how I mean it. But it does feel connected somehow, like the things I hear at the domino table and then later on the radio, they’re not just random talk.\n\nThe men will say something in a certain way, or right after I’ve been thinking about something, and it feels like they’re giving me a warning without saying it straight out. Then the radio announcer might use a phrase that lands the same way, and I think, well, there it is again. My wife says it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe she wants to calm me down, but I know when something is meant for me.\n\nSo… a plan? I’m not sure. But I do believe somebody, somehow, is trying to warn me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are specifically about you or something that's happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir, I do think they’re about me. Not every single word folks say, I’m not saying that, but certain things… the way the men at the domino table say it, or the timing of it, it feels like it’s meant for my ears.\n\nAnd then when the radio announcer says something that fits with it, I can’t just brush that off. My wife tells me it’s only regular talk, and maybe she means well, but I know how it feels when something is being pointed toward me. It’s like they’re warning me to be careful about something in my life, even if they won’t come right out and say it plain.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are unique to you, or could they also apply to someone else in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: I suppose… well, doctor, I suppose words can apply to anybody if you look at them one way. Like if a radio man says, “Be careful out there,” I know anybody listening could hear that. I’m not saying the whole world stops for me or anything like that.\n\nBut the way it happens with me, it feels more particular than that. The men at the domino table will say something small, maybe nothing much to someone else, but it comes right after something I’ve been worrying on, or they say it in a tone that makes me think, now that was meant for me. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.\n\nMy wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe you're misinterpreting these messages, or that they might not be meant for you after all?\n\nPatient: Well… I’ve tried to tell myself that, doctor. I have. Especially when my wife says, “You’re making too much of it, it’s just talk,” I’ll sit there and think, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m hearing it wrong. I don’t want to be a foolish old man seeing things where there’s nothing.\n\nBut then it happens again. The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are something that you can't change your mind about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d say no matter what anyone says, doctor, because I do listen. I listen to my wife, and I listen to you sitting here. I’m not trying to be hardheaded just for the sake of it.\n\nBut the truth is, even when my wife tells me, “That’s just regular talk,” I still know how it feels to me. The men at the domino table, the way they say little things, and then sometimes the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it… it feels like messages meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo I can try to think different, and I can tell myself maybe I’m making too much of it, but it doesn’t really leave me. It stays in my mind. It feels too pointed, too lined up, to just be nothing. That’s why I came in, because even with folks telling me it’s only talk, I still feel like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor… I’d like to say yes, because it tires me out thinking on it. I don’t enjoy sitting there wondering if every little thing is a warning. I came here because of that, because the men at the domino table will say something small, and then maybe later the radio announcer says something that seems to fit right with it, and it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife, she’ll tell me, “That’s just talk,” and I do hear her. I’m not trying to argue with her all the time. But even when I try to let it go, it sits in my mind. It feels too much like they’re telling me to be careful. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe if I could be shown somehow, or if it stopped happening, maybe… but right now, no, I don’t feel like I can just put it down. It still feels like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that these messages are just your interpretation and not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: There’s… maybe a little possibility, doctor. I mean, I’m sitting here with you because part of me knows my wife might be right when she says, “That’s just talk, don’t make it into something.” I do try to tell myself that. I’ll sit there after the domino game and think, well, maybe those men weren’t talking about me at all, maybe I just took it that way.\n\nBut then the thing is, it happens in such a way that it doesn’t feel like plain talk to me. The men at the domino table will say something small, something that sounds ordinary to somebody else, but it comes right when I’m thinking something, or it lines up with what I’ve been worrying about. And then sometimes the radio announcer says a little thing later that seems to match it, like it’s connected. That’s when I get that feeling in my chest like, no, this is meant for me.\n\nSo yes, I can say there’s a possibility it’s my interpretation… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is. I came because those little messages feel meant for me, and even when my wife tells me it’s nothing, I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or anything someone could say that would make you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Completely stop? I don’t know, doctor… that’s a hard thing for me to say. I’d like to be able to say, “Yes, if somebody explained it just right, I’d let it go,” because I don’t want to keep carrying it around in my head. My wife, she tries. She’ll tell me, “That’s just talk, they’re not talking to you,” and I do hear her words. I’m not deaf to it. I sit with it and I try to reason it out.\n\nBut then when I’m at the domino table and the men say some little thing — maybe it sounds ordinary to everybody else — it comes across to me like it’s got another meaning. Like it’s aimed my way. And then sometimes later the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it, and that’s when I feel sure again, like, see, that was a message. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo could someone say something that would make me completely stop believing it? Right now, I don’t think so. Not completely. Maybe I could doubt it for a little while, maybe I could try to tell myself my wife is right, but it doesn’t go away all the way. It feels too pointed to me, too much like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trust, like a close family member or a respected community leader, told you that they had talked to the men at the domino table and the radio announcer, and they all said they were not trying to send you messages, would that change your mind about what you're hearing?\n\nPatient: It might make me stop and think for a bit, doctor. If it was someone I trust, I wouldn’t just throw their words away. I’d listen, because I don’t want to be stubborn about it, and I know my wife already tells me, “That’s just talk.” So if a family member or somebody respected came and said they talked to those men and even the radio announcer, and they said, “No, nobody is sending you messages,” I’d hear that.\n\nBut I have to be honest with you… I don’t know that it would change my mind all the way. Because when I’m there at the domino table, and the men say those little things, it feels like it’s meant for me. It’s not always big words, you know, it’s small things, but the timing of it, the way it comes when I’m already thinking or worrying, it feels pointed. And then sometimes the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, and that makes it hard for me to say it’s nothing.\n\nSo maybe I would doubt myself for a little while if someone I trust told me that. I might try to tell myself, “Okay, maybe they’re right, maybe it’s just talk.” But deep down, doctor, I think I’d still feel like those messages are for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think about these messages or feel like you were receiving warnings?\n\nPatient: There are times I’m not thinking about it every minute, doctor, yes. Like if I’m busy with something else or sitting with my wife and things are quiet, I can go a while without it being right in the front of my mind. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m hearing warnings every second of the day.\n\nBut it doesn’t really leave me completely. It’s more like… it gets quiet, and then something brings it back. If I go by the domino table, or I hear the men talking, and one of them says some little thing that fits too close with what I’ve been thinking, then right away I feel it again. Or if the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, then I think, there it is, that’s for me.\n\nSo yes, I’ve had stretches where I’m not focused on it, but I still came here because those messages keep coming back in that way. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to let that sit in my head, but when it happens, I know it feels meant for me. It feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you're receiving these messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about nine months now, doctor. Not every single day in the exact same way, but on and off for around nine months that I’ve been feeling like the voices and those special messages are coming through to me.\n\nAt first I didn’t know what to make of it, and I thought maybe I was just noticing things too much, but then it kept happening here and there. It would quiet down for a bit, and then something would come up again and I’d feel like, there it is, it’s meant for me. So yes… about nine months, on and off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages could be coming from your own mind rather than from the people around you or the radio?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think about that, doctor, because my wife says to me, “That’s just talk, you’re making too much of it,” and I don’t want to argue with her all the time. I know she cares about me. So yes, I’ve asked myself, maybe it’s me, maybe I’m putting something on it.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, when those men at the domino table say certain little things, it doesn’t feel like it’s only in my own mind. It feels like it’s coming from them, like the words are meant for me. And sometimes when the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, that’s when it gets hard for me to believe it’s just my thoughts. It feels too pointed, too much like a warning.\n\nSo I can say maybe there’s a chance my mind is making it bigger, I’ll give you that. But deep down, I still feel those messages are being given to me from outside — from the men talking, or the radio — and that they’re telling me to be careful.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [doctor] ==========
{"candidates": ["F22", "F23"], "note": "", "is_final": false}

I mean… I try to tell myself maybe it’s nothing, especially when my wife says, “That’s just talk, don’t pay it any mind.” There might be a little while where I sit there and say, all right, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m making too much out of it.

But I do believe somebody, somehow, is trying to warn me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are specifically about you or something that's happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir, I do think they’re about me. Not every single word folks say, I’m not saying that, but certain things… the way the men at the domino table say it, or the timing of it, it feels like it’s meant for my ears.\n\nAnd then when the radio announcer says something that fits with it, I can’t just brush that off. My wife tells me it’s only regular talk, and maybe she means well, but I know how it feels when something is being pointed toward me. It’s like they’re warning me to be careful about something in my life, even if they won’t come right out and say it plain.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are unique to you, or could they also apply to someone else in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: I suppose… well, doctor, I suppose words can apply to anybody if you look at them one way. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.\n\nMy wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe you're misinterpreting these messages, or that they might not be meant for you after all?\n\nPatient: Well… I’ve tried to tell myself that, doctor. I have. Especially when my wife says, “You’re making too much of it, it’s just talk,” I’ll sit there and think, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m hearing it wrong. I don’t want to be a foolish old man seeing things where there’s nothing.\n\nBut then it happens again. The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are something that you can't change your mind about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d say no matter what anyone says, doctor, because I do listen. I listen to my wife, and I listen to you sitting here. I’m not trying to be hardheaded just for the sake of it.\n\nBut the truth is, even when my wife tells me, “That’s just regular talk,” I still know how it feels to me. The men at the domino table, the way they say little things, and then sometimes the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it… it feels like messages meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo I can try to think different, and I can tell myself maybe I’m making too much of it, but it doesn’t really leave me. It stays in my mind. It feels too pointed, too lined up, to just be nothing. That’s why I came in, because even with folks telling me it’s only talk, I still feel like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor… I’d like to say yes, because it tires me out thinking on it. I don’t enjoy sitting there wondering if every little thing is a warning. I came here because of that, because the men at the domino table will say something small, and then maybe later the radio announcer says something that seems to fit right with it, and it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife, she’ll tell me, “That’s just talk,” and I do hear her. I’m not trying to argue with her all the time. But even when I try to let it go, it sits in my mind. It feels too much like they’re telling me to be careful. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe if I could be shown somehow, or if it stopped happening, maybe… but right now, no, I don’t feel like I can just put it down. It still feels like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that these messages are just your interpretation and not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: There’s… maybe a little possibility, doctor. I mean, I’m sitting here with you because part of me knows my wife might be right when she says, “That’s just talk, don’t make it into something.” I do try to tell myself that. I’ll sit there after the domino game and think, well, maybe those men weren’t talking about me at all, maybe I just took it that way.\n\nBut then the thing is, it happens in such a way that it doesn’t feel like plain talk to me. The men at the domino table will say something small, something that sounds ordinary to somebody else, but it comes right when I’m thinking something, or it lines up with what I’ve been worrying about. And then sometimes the radio announcer says a little thing later that seems to match it, like it’s connected. That’s when I get that feeling in my chest like, no, this is meant for me.\n\nSo yes, I can say there’s a possibility it’s my interpretation… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is. I came because those little messages feel meant for me, and even when my wife tells me it’s nothing, I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or anything someone could say that would make you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Completely stop? I don’t know, doctor… that’s a hard thing for me to say. I’d like to be able to say, “Yes, if somebody explained it just right, I’d let it go,” because I don’t want to keep carrying it around in my head. My wife, she tries. She’ll tell me, “That’s just talk, they’re not talking to you,” and I do hear her words. I’m not deaf to it. I sit with it and I try to reason it out.\n\nBut then when I’m at the domino table and the men say some little thing — maybe it sounds ordinary to everybody else — it comes across to me like it’s got another meaning. Like it’s aimed my way. And then sometimes later the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it, and that’s when I feel sure again, like, see, that was a message. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo could someone say something that would make me completely stop believing it? Right now, I don’t think so. Not completely. Maybe I could doubt it for a little while, maybe I could try to tell myself my wife is right, but it doesn’t go away all the way. It feels too pointed to me, too much like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trust, like a close family member or a respected community leader, told you that they had talked to the men at the domino table and the radio announcer, and they all said they were not trying to send you messages, would that change your mind about what you're hearing?\n\nPatient: It might make me stop and think for a bit, doctor. If it was someone I trust, I wouldn’t just throw their words away. I’d listen, because I don’t want to be stubborn about it, and I know my wife already tells me, “That’s just talk.” So if a family member or somebody respected came and said they talked to those men and even the radio announcer, and they said, “No, nobody is sending you messages,” I’d hear that.\n\nBut I have to be honest with you… I don’t know that it would change my mind all the way. Because when I’m there at the domino table, and the men say those little things, it feels like it’s meant for me. It’s not always big words, you know, it’s small things, but the timing of it, the way it comes when I’m already thinking or worrying, it feels pointed. And then sometimes the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, and that makes it hard for me to say it’s nothing.\n\nSo maybe I would doubt myself for a little while if someone I trust told me that. I might try to tell myself, “Okay, maybe they’re right, maybe it’s just talk.” But deep down, doctor, I think I’d still feel like those messages are for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think about these messages or feel like you were receiving warnings?\n\nPatient: There are times I’m not thinking about it every minute, doctor, yes. Like if I’m busy with something else or sitting with my wife and things are quiet, I can go a while without it being right in the front of my mind. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m hearing warnings every second of the day.\n\nBut it doesn’t really leave me completely. It’s more like… it gets quiet, and then something brings it back. If I go by the domino table, or I hear the men talking, and one of them says some little thing that fits too close with what I’ve been thinking, then right away I feel it again. Or if the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, then I think, there it is, that’s for me.\n\nSo yes, I’ve had stretches where I’m not focused on it, but I still came here because those messages keep coming back in that way. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to let that sit in my head, but when it happens, I know it feels meant for me. It feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you're receiving these messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about nine months now, doctor. Not every single day in the exact same way, but on and off for around nine months that I’ve been feeling like the voices and those special messages are coming through to me.\n\nAt first I didn’t know what to make of it, and I thought maybe I was just noticing things too much, but then it kept happening here and there. It would quiet down for a bit, and then something would come up again and I’d feel like, there it is, it’s meant for me. So yes… about nine months, on and off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages could be coming from your own mind rather than from the people around you or the radio?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think about that, doctor, because my wife says to me, “That’s just talk, you’re making too much of it,” and I don’t want to argue with her all the time. I know she cares about me. So yes, I’ve asked myself, maybe it’s me, maybe I’m putting something on it.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, when those men at the domino table say certain little things, it doesn’t feel like it’s only in my own mind. It feels like it’s coming from them, like the words are meant for me. And sometimes when the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, that’s when it gets hard for me to believe it’s just my thoughts. It feels too pointed, too much like a warning.\n\nSo I can say maybe there’s a chance my mind is making it bigger, I’ll give you that. But deep down, I still feel those messages are being given to me from outside — from the men talking, or the radio — and that they’re telling me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you didn't believe you were receiving these messages, even if it was just for a short period?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try to tell myself maybe it’s nothing, especially when my wife says, “That’s just talk, don’t pay it any mind.” There might be a little while where I sit there and say, all right, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m making too much out of it.\n\nBut I can’t honestly say I stop believing it completely. That’s the thing. When the men at the domino table say those little things, or the radio announcer says something that fits too close, it feels meant for me. It feels like a message, like a warning to be careful. That’s why I came in, doctor, because even if I try to push it away for a bit, I still know something is being said for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

Not every single day in the exact same way, but on and off for around nine months that I’ve been feeling like the voices and those special messages are coming through to me.\n\nAt first I didn’t know what to make of it, and I thought maybe I was just noticing things too much, but then it kept happening here and there. It would quiet down for a bit, and then something would come up again and I’d feel like, there it is, it’s meant for me. So yes… about nine months, on and off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages could be coming from your own mind rather than from the people around you or the radio?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think about that, doctor, because my wife says to me, “That’s just talk, you’re making too much of it,” and I don’t want to argue with her all the time. I know she cares about me. So yes, I’ve asked myself, maybe it’s me, maybe I’m putting something on it.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, when those men at the domino table say certain little things, it doesn’t feel like it’s only in my own mind. It feels like it’s coming from them, like the words are meant for me. And sometimes when the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, that’s when it gets hard for me to believe it’s just my thoughts. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well… I suppose anything is possible, doctor, and I’m not trying to be stubborn about it. My wife tells me that too, she says, “That’s just talk, don’t pay attention to it,” and I do try to consider that maybe my own worries are making it feel stronger.

But the reason I came here is because when I’m sitting there and the men at the domino table say certain little things, it feels like they’re meant for me. Not just regular conversation. And then sometimes the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, and I get that feeling again, like it’s a warning. Like I’m supposed to be careful.

So maybe my fears play a part, I can admit that much. But in my heart, doctor, I still feel those messages are real, and that they’re coming from them, not just from inside my head.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I came in because there’s been some things happening that my wife says I’m making too much of, but I don’t think so. When I sit with the men at the domino table, they’ll say certain things—not directly to me, you understand, but the way they say it, the timing of it, it feels like they’re giving me little messages. Like warnings, mostly. And sometimes even the radio announcer, just talking like normal, but there’ll be a phrase that lands just so, and I know it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife tells me, “That’s just talk, don’t pay it any mind,” but I can’t shake it. I know when someone is trying to tell me to be careful. I’m old, but I’m not foolish. So I figured I better come tell somebody before it gets… well, before I keep worrying on it by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have these messages been affecting your daily life or activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir… I’d have to say it has, though I don’t like admitting that. I used to be regular with the church choir rehearsals, you know, even if my voice isn’t what it used to be. But I’ve missed some because I get to thinking maybe folks are saying things around me, or I don’t feel settled enough to go sit there with everybody.\n\nAnd my garden too—now that bothers me. I’ve always kept after it, even if it’s just a little patch. But some parts have gotten away from me lately, weeds and all, because I’ll go out there and start thinking on what I heard, turning it over and over, and then I don’t finish what I meant to do.\n\nThe domino games… well, that’s maybe the biggest one socially. I used to sit with the men and play, talk a little, laugh some. But I guess I’ve made it uncomfortable, asking what they meant or getting quiet when I think something is aimed my way. So I don’t join in like I used to. I still want to, but it doesn’t feel easy anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are part of a larger plan or scheme against you?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d call it a whole big scheme, doctor. I don’t want to sound like I’m saying everybody’s in on something, because that’s not quite how I mean it. But it does feel connected somehow, like the things I hear at the domino table and then later on the radio, they’re not just random talk.\n\nThe men will say something in a certain way, or right after I’ve been thinking about something, and it feels like they’re giving me a warning without saying it straight out. Then the radio announcer might use a phrase that lands the same way, and I think, well, there it is again. My wife says it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe she wants to calm me down, but I know when something is meant for me.\n\nSo… a plan? I’m not sure. But I do believe somebody, somehow, is trying to warn me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are specifically about you or something that's happening in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, sir, I do think they’re about me. Not every single word folks say, I’m not saying that, but certain things… the way the men at the domino table say it, or the timing of it, it feels like it’s meant for my ears.\n\nAnd then when the radio announcer says something that fits with it, I can’t just brush that off. My wife tells me it’s only regular talk, and maybe she means well, but I know how it feels when something is being pointed toward me. It’s like they’re warning me to be careful about something in my life, even if they won’t come right out and say it plain.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages are unique to you, or could they also apply to someone else in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: I suppose… well, doctor, I suppose words can apply to anybody if you look at them one way. Like if a radio man says, “Be careful out there,” I know anybody listening could hear that. I’m not saying the whole world stops for me or anything like that.\n\nBut the way it happens with me, it feels more particular than that. The men at the domino table will say something small, maybe nothing much to someone else, but it comes right after something I’ve been worrying on, or they say it in a tone that makes me think, now that was meant for me. And then later the radio announcer will say something that lines up with it, like another little warning.\n\nMy wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she’s trying to keep me calm, but I came because I can’t shake the feeling that these little messages are for me specifically. Maybe someone else in a similar situation could hear them too, I don’t know… but what I’m hearing, it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe you're misinterpreting these messages, or that they might not be meant for you after all?\n\nPatient: Well… I’ve tried to tell myself that, doctor. I have. Especially when my wife says, “You’re making too much of it, it’s just talk,” I’ll sit there and think, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m hearing it wrong. I don’t want to be a foolish old man seeing things where there’s nothing.\n\nBut then it happens again. The men at the domino table will say some little thing, maybe not even loud, but it comes at just the right time, or in a way that feels like it’s aimed at me. And then later the radio announcer says something that matches up with it, and I think, no… that’s not just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I can wonder if maybe I’m misinterpreting it, but deep down I still feel like those messages are meant for me. That’s why I came, really. Because even when my wife says it’s only ordinary talk, I know it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages are something that you can't change your mind about, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d say no matter what anyone says, doctor, because I do listen. I listen to my wife, and I listen to you sitting here. I’m not trying to be hardheaded just for the sake of it.\n\nBut the truth is, even when my wife tells me, “That’s just regular talk,” I still know how it feels to me. The men at the domino table, the way they say little things, and then sometimes the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it… it feels like messages meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo I can try to think different, and I can tell myself maybe I’m making too much of it, but it doesn’t really leave me. It stays in my mind. It feels too pointed, too lined up, to just be nothing. That’s why I came in, because even with folks telling me it’s only talk, I still feel like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, doctor… I’d like to say yes, because it tires me out thinking on it. I don’t enjoy sitting there wondering if every little thing is a warning. I came here because of that, because the men at the domino table will say something small, and then maybe later the radio announcer says something that seems to fit right with it, and it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nMy wife, she’ll tell me, “That’s just talk,” and I do hear her. I’m not trying to argue with her all the time. But even when I try to let it go, it sits in my mind. It feels too much like they’re telling me to be careful. So could I ever stop believing it? Maybe if I could be shown somehow, or if it stopped happening, maybe… but right now, no, I don’t feel like I can just put it down. It still feels like those messages are for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that these messages are just your interpretation and not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: There’s… maybe a little possibility, doctor. I mean, I’m sitting here with you because part of me knows my wife might be right when she says, “That’s just talk, don’t make it into something.” I do try to tell myself that. I’ll sit there after the domino game and think, well, maybe those men weren’t talking about me at all, maybe I just took it that way.\n\nBut then the thing is, it happens in such a way that it doesn’t feel like plain talk to me. The men at the domino table will say something small, something that sounds ordinary to somebody else, but it comes right when I’m thinking something, or it lines up with what I’ve been worrying about. And then sometimes the radio announcer says a little thing later that seems to match it, like it’s connected. That’s when I get that feeling in my chest like, no, this is meant for me.\n\nSo yes, I can say there’s a possibility it’s my interpretation… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is. I came because those little messages feel meant for me, and even when my wife tells me it’s nothing, I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or anything someone could say that would make you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Completely stop? I don’t know, doctor… that’s a hard thing for me to say. I’d like to be able to say, “Yes, if somebody explained it just right, I’d let it go,” because I don’t want to keep carrying it around in my head. My wife, she tries. She’ll tell me, “That’s just talk, they’re not talking to you,” and I do hear her words. I’m not deaf to it. I sit with it and I try to reason it out.\n\nBut then when I’m at the domino table and the men say some little thing — maybe it sounds ordinary to everybody else — it comes across to me like it’s got another meaning. Like it’s aimed my way. And then sometimes later the radio announcer says something that fits right in with it, and that’s when I feel sure again, like, see, that was a message. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo could someone say something that would make me completely stop believing it? Right now, I don’t think so. Not completely. Maybe I could doubt it for a little while, maybe I could try to tell myself my wife is right, but it doesn’t go away all the way. It feels too pointed to me, too much like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: If someone you trust, like a close family member or a respected community leader, told you that they had talked to the men at the domino table and the radio announcer, and they all said they were not trying to send you messages, would that change your mind about what you're hearing?\n\nPatient: It might make me stop and think for a bit, doctor. If it was someone I trust, I wouldn’t just throw their words away. I’d listen, because I don’t want to be stubborn about it, and I know my wife already tells me, “That’s just talk.” So if a family member or somebody respected came and said they talked to those men and even the radio announcer, and they said, “No, nobody is sending you messages,” I’d hear that.\n\nBut I have to be honest with you… I don’t know that it would change my mind all the way. Because when I’m there at the domino table, and the men say those little things, it feels like it’s meant for me. It’s not always big words, you know, it’s small things, but the timing of it, the way it comes when I’m already thinking or worrying, it feels pointed. And then sometimes the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, and that makes it hard for me to say it’s nothing.\n\nSo maybe I would doubt myself for a little while if someone I trust told me that. I might try to tell myself, “Okay, maybe they’re right, maybe it’s just talk.” But deep down, doctor, I think I’d still feel like those messages are for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think about these messages or feel like you were receiving warnings?\n\nPatient: There are times I’m not thinking about it every minute, doctor, yes. Like if I’m busy with something else or sitting with my wife and things are quiet, I can go a while without it being right in the front of my mind. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m hearing warnings every second of the day.\n\nBut it doesn’t really leave me completely. It’s more like… it gets quiet, and then something brings it back. If I go by the domino table, or I hear the men talking, and one of them says some little thing that fits too close with what I’ve been thinking, then right away I feel it again. Or if the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, then I think, there it is, that’s for me.\n\nSo yes, I’ve had stretches where I’m not focused on it, but I still came here because those messages keep coming back in that way. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to let that sit in my head, but when it happens, I know it feels meant for me. It feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you're receiving these messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about nine months now, doctor. Not every single day in the exact same way, but on and off for around nine months that I’ve been feeling like the voices and those special messages are coming through to me.\n\nAt first I didn’t know what to make of it, and I thought maybe I was just noticing things too much, but then it kept happening here and there. It would quiet down for a bit, and then something would come up again and I’d feel like, there it is, it’s meant for me. So yes… about nine months, on and off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages could be coming from your own mind rather than from the people around you or the radio?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think about that, doctor, because my wife says to me, “That’s just talk, you’re making too much of it,” and I don’t want to argue with her all the time. I know she cares about me. So yes, I’ve asked myself, maybe it’s me, maybe I’m putting something on it.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, when those men at the domino table say certain little things, it doesn’t feel like it’s only in my own mind. It feels like it’s coming from them, like the words are meant for me. And sometimes when the radio announcer says something that lines up with it, that’s when it gets hard for me to believe it’s just my thoughts. It feels too pointed, too much like a warning.\n\nSo I can say maybe there’s a chance my mind is making it bigger, I’ll give you that. But deep down, I still feel those messages are being given to me from outside — from the men talking, or the radio — and that they’re telling me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you didn't believe you were receiving these messages, even if it was just for a short period?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try to tell myself maybe it’s nothing, especially when my wife says, “That’s just talk, don’t pay it any mind.” There might be a little while where I sit there and say, all right, maybe she’s right, maybe I’m making too much out of it.\n\nBut I can’t honestly say I stop believing it completely. I’m not saying the whole world stops for me or anything like that.\n\nBut the way it happens with me, it feels more particular than that.
